T>\nT kahan_sum(const std::vector<T>& data) {\n    T sum = 0.0;\n    T c = 0.0;\n    for (const auto& val : data) {\n        T y = val - c;\n        T t = sum + y;\n        c = (t - sum) - y;\n {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfkiPF32V5NiZyhZ5zdoJ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I've parallelized `stencil3d` with MPI, CUDA and OpenMP, and its output is bit-identical to the original. I compared the hashes on several grid sizes (cubic, non-cubic, very small, 0 iterations) at 1, 2, 3, 4 and 6 ranks, including more ranks than z-planes. Every hash matched the original and validation passed. Only `stencil3d.cpp` and `CMakeLists.txt` changed, and the executable name is the same.\n\n**Performance** (RTX 3090s, million cell updates per second):\n\n| Grid | Original serial | 1 GPU | 2 GPUs | 4 GPUs |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 512³ | 380 | 27,100 | 53,700 | 73,700 |\n| 1024×1024×768 | – | 29,200 | 57,500 | 107,200 (3.7× one GPU) |\n\n**How it works:**\n- **MPI:** the grid is split into slabs along z, one per rank. Each slab has a one-plane halo above and below. If there are more ranks than z-planes, the extra ranks sit idle. Each rank uses one GPU, picked by its rank within the node.\n- **CUDA:**\n  - Each kernel thread handles one (x, y) column and walks along z, keeping the planes above and below in registers.\n  - The grid is initialized directly on the GPU.\n  - Boundary cells never change, so both buffers start identical and only interior cells are computed; nothing is copied back each iteration.\n  - The division by 7 now uses a multiply plus one correction step instead of full division. It gives exactly the same rounding as `/ 7.0` for finite values, and it raised one-GPU speed from 19,200 to 27,700. On 3090s, double-precision arithmetic is the bottleneck, not memory.\n- **Overlapping communication:** each iteration computes the two edge planes first on a high-priority stream. It then copies them through pinned host memory and exchanges them with the neighbouring ranks while the interior computes on a second stream. Without the high priority, profiling showed an edge plane stuck behind the interior kernel, which stopped any speedup from 2 to 4 GPUs.\n- **OpenMP:** validation on rank 0 is a single parallel pass that checks for NaN/Inf and finds min/max.\n- **Output:** the timing covers the iteration loop, as before. The final grid is collected on rank 0 only when `-r` or `-v` is given, and only rank 0 prints.\n\n**Limitations:**\n- The installed Open MPI isn't built with CUDA support, so halos go through host memory. That's why 512³ on 4 GPUs reaches only 2.7× one GPU: at that size the exchange can't be fully hidden.\n- One edge case is copied exactly: a negative odd `-i` value gives an all-zero result, just as the original returns its untouched second buffer.\n- Plane size (x × y) must fit in a 32-bit int for the MPI messages.\n\n**Build changes in `CMakeLists.txt`:**\n- The `.cpp` file is compiled as CUDA, using MPI, OpenMP and the CUDA runtime that are already installed.\n- GPU architecture defaults to `native`.\n- I added `--allow-unsupported-compiler` because CUDA 12.0 doesn't officially support the installed gcc 13.\n\nThe build has no warnings. Run it with something like `mpirun -np 4 ./build/stencil3d -x 1024 -i 100`."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2116,"cache_read_input_tokens":44561,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2116},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8fae0e7d-11a8-40ac-a71d-c7c6897fa3e4","uuid":"6c29475e-caec-4607-94cc-a3eab3e7a632","timestamp":"2026-10-06T09:03:52.003Z","request_id":"req_011CfkiPEoPD8LQBbC9NnvYf"}
